\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda+KR^{-2})

\displaystyle\qquad+C_{s,U}R^{d}L^{d-1}e^{d\frac{3-2s}{1-s}\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}(1+\max\{\lambda,0\}+\llbracket\mathcal{Z}\rrbracket_{H^{s}(U)}^{\frac{1}{1-s}})^{\frac{d}{2}}.

Proof.

(a)

According to
[
18
, Lemma 8.2]
, there exists a smooth function
\eta:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to[0,1]
and a
K>0
, such that
\eta=1
on
[0,L-2l]^{d}
,

\operatorname{supp}(\eta)\subseteq[-2l,L]^{d}
,

\lVert\nabla\eta\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{2}\leq\frac{K}{2^{d}l^{2}}
and

\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\eta(x+kL)^{2}=1\quad\mbox{ for }x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

We set
\eta_{k}\vcentcolon=\eta(\,\cdot+(l,l,\ldots,l)+Lk)
for
k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}
.
Observe that
\operatorname{supp}(\eta_{k})\subseteq kL+[-l,L+l]^{d}
, and,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\eta_{k}^{2}=1\mbox{ on }[0,nL]^{d},\qquad\Big\|\sum_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}|\nabla\eta_{k}|^{2}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq\frac{K}{l^{2}}.

Therefore, the map

\Phi:L^{2}([0,nL]^{d})\rightarrow\bigoplus_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}L^{2}(kL+[-l,L+l]^{d}),\quad u\mapsto(\eta_{k}u)_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}.

is an isometry and
\Phi(e^{W}H^{1}_{0}([0,nL]^{d}))\subseteq\oplus_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}e^{W}H^{1}_{0}(kL+[-l,L+l]^{d})
.

Observe that for
u\in H^{1}_{0}([0,nL]^{d})
, for
\phi\in C_{c}^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
such that
\phi=1
on a neighborhood of
[0,nL]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}_{Z}^{[0,nL]^{d}}(u,u)=\langle\phi Z,\mathbbm{1}_{[0,nL]^{d}}u^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\langle\phi Z,\mathbbm{1}_{[0,nL]^{d}}\eta_{k}^{2}u^{2}\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\mathcal{Z}_{Z}^{kL+[-l,L+l]^{d}}(\eta_{k}u,\eta_{k}u).

Therefore, by Lemma
4.26
,

\mathcal{E}_{W,\mathcal{Z}}^{[0,nL]^{d}}(u,u)\geq\sum_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}\Big\{\mathcal{E}_{W,\mathcal{Z}}^{kL+[-l,L+l]^{d}}(\eta_{k}u,\eta_{k}u)-\frac{K}{l^{2}}\lVert\eta_{k}u\rVert_{L^{2}(kL+[-l,L+l]^{d})}^{2}\Big\}.

and thus
\mathcal{E}_{W,\mathcal{Z}}^{[0,nL]^{d}}\succ\bigoplus_{k\in[-1,n+1]^{d}\cap\mathbb{Z}^{d}}[\mathcal{E}_{W,\mathcal{Z}}^{kL+[-l,L+l]^{d}}-\frac{K}{l^{2}}\mathfrak{I}]
(where
\mathfrak{I}
is as in Definition
4.23
), from which we conclude the estimates (use the discussion in Definition
4.23
).

(b)

As given in
[
21
, Proposition 4.3]
, there exist smooth functions
\alpha_{1}
and
\alpha_{2}
on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and a
K>0
(only depending on
d
) such that

\displaystyle\operatorname{supp}(\alpha_{1})\subseteq U_{L}\setminus B(\partial U_{L},\tfrac{R}{2}),\quad\operatorname{supp}(\alpha_{2})\subseteq B(\partial U_{L},R),

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{2}+\alpha_{2}^{2}=1\mbox{ on a neighborhood of }U_{L},\qquad\Big\|\sum_{j=1}^{2}\lvert\nabla\alpha_{j}\rvert^{2}\Big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq KR^{-2}.

Recall the definitions of
U_{L}^{R}
and
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m},R}(U^{R}_{L})
from Definition
4.17
.
The map

\Phi:L^{2}(U_{L})\rightarrow L^{2}(U_{L})\oplus L^{2}(U^{R}_{L}),\quad u\mapsto\alpha_{1}u\oplus\alpha_{2}u

is an isometry and
\Phi(e^{W}H^{1}(U_{L}))\subseteq e^{W}H^{1}_{0}(U_{L})\oplus e^{W}H^{1}_{\mathfrak{m},R}(U^{R}_{L})
.

Observe that
\mathcal{Z}^{U_{L}}(\alpha_{2}u,\alpha_{2}u)=\mathcal{Z}^{U_{L}^{R}}(\alpha_{2}u,\alpha_{2}u)
as
\operatorname{supp}\alpha_{2}\cap U_{L}\subseteq U_{L}^{R}
.
Therefore, by Lemma
4.26
, for
u\in H^{1}(U_{L})
,

\mathcal{E}^{U_{L}}(u,u)\geq\mathcal{E}^{U_{L}}(\alpha_{1}u,\alpha_{1}u)+\mathcal{E}^{U_{L}^{R}}(\alpha_{2}u,\alpha_{2}u)-\sum_{j=1}^{2}KR^{-2}\lVert\alpha_{j}u\rVert_{L^{2}(U_{L})}^{2}.

By applying Lemma
4.7
with
\delta=\frac{e^{-2\|W\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}}{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{U_{L}^{R}}(\alpha_{2}u,\alpha_{2}u)

\displaystyle\geq\frac{e^{-2\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}}{2}\int_{U^{R}_{L}}\lvert\nabla(\alpha_{2}u^{\flat})\rvert^{2}-A\lVert\alpha_{2}u\rVert^{2}_{L^{2}(U^{R}_{L})},

where

A\vcentcolon=\frac{e^{-2\|W\|_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}}{2}+2^{\frac{s}{1-s}}e^{\frac{2s}{1-s}\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}C_{\operatorname{IP}}^{U_{L}}[H^{s}]^{\frac{2}{1-s}}\llbracket\mathcal{Z}\rrbracket_{H^{s}(U)}^{\frac{1}{1-s}}.

Therefore,

\mathcal{E}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U_{L}}\succ(\mathcal{E}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U_{L}}-KR^{-2}\mathfrak{I})+(\mathcal{E}^{\mathfrak{m},R,U_{L}^{R}}-(KR^{-2}+A)\mathfrak{I})
, where
\mathcal{E}^{\mathfrak{m},R,U_{L}^{R}}
is the restriction of
\mathcal{E}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U_{L}^{R}}
to
H_{\mathfrak{m},R}(U_{L}^{R})
, and thus, by Lemma
4.22
(the additional factor
e^{2\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}
is explained similarly as in (
50
)),

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda+KR^{-2})+\bm{N}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}(U^{R}_{L},2e^{4\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U_{L})}}(\lambda+KR^{-2}+A)).

By Lemma
4.20

(a)
, for
R\geq R_{U}
,

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathfrak{m}}_{0}(U^{R}_{L},2e^{4\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U)}}(\lambda+KR^{-2}+A))

\displaystyle\qquad\lesssim_{U}R^{d}L^{d-1}\big\{e^{4\lVert W\rVert_{L^{\infty}(U)}}(1+\max\{\lambda,0\}+KR^{-2}+A)\big\}^{\frac{d}{2}}.

It remains to apply Lemma
A.18
, more specifically (
82
):
C^{U_{L}}_{\operatorname{IP}}[H^{s}]\lesssim_{s,U}1
.
∎

5
The Anderson Hamiltonian with Dirichlet and Neumann boundary conditions

Based on the results obtained Section
4
we can give the definition of the Anderson Hamiltonian

-\Delta-\xi
with Dirichlet and with Neumann boundary conditions, and show that it is the limit of the operators
-\Delta-\xi_{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}
, where
the
c_{\varepsilon}
’s are as in Assumption
3.2

(I)
.

We discuss the construction of the Anderson Hamiltonian with Dirichlet boundary conditions in Section
5.1
and with Neumann boundary conditions in Section
5.2
.
In Section
5.3
we consider the integrated density of states associated to the Anderson Hamiltonian.

5.1
The Dirichlet Anderson Hamiltonian

Assumptions 5.1

(for this section)
.

In this section we impose the construction assumption
3.2

(I)
.

As discussed in Remark
3.1
,
we will choose
M
to be a random variable with values in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
F(W_{M}^{\varepsilon})=e^{2W_{M}^{\varepsilon}}
, where
F
is as in Assumption
3.2

(I)
:

Definition 5.2
.

Let
U
be a bounded domain and let
r\in(1-\delta,1)
. Using the notation of Theorem
4.3

(a)
, for
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define the following symmetric forms on
H_{0}^{r}(U)
:

\mathcal{Z}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}^{U}_{Y_{N}},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}^{U}_{Y^{\varepsilon}_{N}}.

(54)

For
\delta_{-}\in(0,\delta]
and
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
, we set

M(U,\delta_{-};\gamma)\vcentcolon=\inf\{N\in\mathbb{N}\nonscript\>:\nonscript\>\mathopen{}\lVert W_{n}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta_{-}}(U)}\leq\gamma\mbox{ for all }n\geq N\},

(55)

which is finite by Lemma
2.11
.
Recalling the notation from Definition
4.8
, for

M=M(U,\delta;1)
(which attains its values in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
by Lemma
2.11
), we define

\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}_{W_{M},\mathcal{Z}_{M}[U]}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U},\quad\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}_{W^{\varepsilon}_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{M}[U]}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}.

Recalling Proposition
4.10
, we set

\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)\vcentcolon=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U;W_{M},\mathcal{Z}_{M}[U]),\quad\lambda_{k;\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)\vcentcolon=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U;W^{\varepsilon}_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{M}[U]).

Theorem 5.3
.

For
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=-\Delta-\xi_{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}.

(56)

Let
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
be a sequence in
(0,1)
such that
\varepsilon_{n}\to 0
.
Then, there exist a subsequence
(\varepsilon_{n_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and a subset

\Omega_{1}\subseteq\Omega
of
\mathbb{P}
-probability
1
such that on
\Omega_{1}
the following holds:
for any bounded domain
U
, one has

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}\xrightarrow{\textnormal{NR}}_{m\rightarrow\infty}\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U},

(57)

for all
k\in\mathbb{N}

\lim_{m\to\infty}\lambda_{k;\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U),

and there exist an eigenfunction
\phi_{k}
of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}
corresponding to
\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
and eigenfunctions
\phi_{k,m}
of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}},U}_{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}
corresponding to
\lambda_{k;\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
such that

\displaystyle\phi_{k,m}\xrightarrow{m\to\infty}\phi_{k}\quad\mbox{ in }L^{2}(U).

Proof.

(
56
) follows by our choice of
M
(see (
55
)) and by Lemma
1.15
and
the construction assumption
3.2

(I)
(see also the discussion in Section
1.5
and Remark
3.1
).

Let
\sigma\in(0,1)
.
By
3.2

(I)
,
there exist a subsequence
(\varepsilon_{n_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and a subset

\Omega_{1}\subseteq\Omega
of
\mathbb{P}
-probability
1
such that on
\Omega_{1}
, for every
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,

\displaystyle\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\lVert X^{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}-X\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}=0,\qquad\lim_{m\rightarrow\infty}\lVert Y^{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}_{N}-Y_{N}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Observe that by Lemma
A.5
and Corollary
A.10
, because
W_{M}^{\varepsilon}-W_{M}=G_{M}*(X^{\varepsilon}-X)
, for all
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\lVert W^{\varepsilon}_{M}-W_{M}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(U)}\lesssim_{U,\delta,\sigma}\lVert X^{\varepsilon}-X\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

and that by Theorem
4.3

(a)
, for
r\in(1-\delta,1)
,

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{M}-\mathcal{Z}_{M}\rrbracket_{H^{r}_{0}(U)}\lesssim_{\delta,p,U}\lVert Y^{\varepsilon}_{M}-Y_{M}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Therefore, the claim follows from Theorem
4.14
and Lemma
4.12
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Let
\mathfrak{M}
be a random variable with values in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\mathfrak{M}\geq M(U,\delta;1)
.
Then, almost surely,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)=\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}_{W_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}(U)
, because
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}_{W^{\varepsilon}_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}(U)=-\Delta-\xi_{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)
and similarly as in Theorem
5.3
,
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}_{W_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}(U)
is the limit (in the sense of (
57
)) of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}_{W^{\varepsilon}_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}(U)
.
We will apply this in Section
5.3
with
\mathfrak{M}=M(U,\delta;\gamma)
for
\gamma\in(0,1]
.

5.2
The Neumann Anderson Hamiltonian

As described in the beginning of Section
4
(below Lemma
1.15
), the boundary term will be dealt with by the decomposition into symmetric forms
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}
and
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}
. Let us first consider the ingredients for the latter symmetric form.

Definition 5.5
.

Let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain.
For
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
we define

\displaystyle\widehat{Y}_{N}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=G_{N}\ast(X-\xi)+(G_{N}-G_{0})\ast\xi,

\displaystyle\widehat{Y}^{\varepsilon}_{N}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=G_{N}\ast(X^{\varepsilon}-\xi_{\varepsilon})+(G_{N}-G_{0})\ast\xi_{\varepsilon},\qquad\varepsilon\in(0,1).

Lemma 5.6
.

Let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain. Then for
\delta\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
and
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{Y}_{N}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\delta,\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{U,\delta}\lVert X-\xi\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+2\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+2^{N}\lVert\xi\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

\displaystyle\lVert\widehat{Y}_{N}-\widehat{Y}^{\varepsilon}_{N}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\delta,\sigma}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{U,\delta}\lVert X-\xi-(X^{\varepsilon}-\xi_{\varepsilon})\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+2\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+2^{N}\lVert\xi-\xi_{\varepsilon}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

Moreover,
\lVert\widehat{Y}_{N}-\widehat{Y}^{\varepsilon}_{N}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\delta,\sigma}}
converges in probability to
0
.

Proof.

The estimates follow by
the construction assumption
3.2

(I)

and Corollary
A.10
.
∎

Definition 5.7
.

Impose the construction and Neumann assumptions,
3.2

(I)
and
(II)
,
let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain.
Let
r\in(1-\delta,1)
.
Using the notation of Theorem
4.3
,we define the following symmetric forms on
H^{r}(U)
, for
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}[U]

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\tilde{Y}^{U},e^{2W_{N}}}^{U},\qquad\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\tilde{Y}^{U}_{\varepsilon},e^{2W^{\varepsilon}_{N}}}^{U},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}[U]

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{U}_{\widehat{Y}_{N},e^{2W_{N}}},\qquad\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{U}_{\widehat{Y}^{\varepsilon}_{N},e^{2W^{\varepsilon}_{N}}}.

We furthermore make abuse of notation (compared to the symmetric forms on
H_{0}^{r}(U)
as in (
54
)) and define the following symmetric forms on
H^{r}(U)
,

\mathcal{Z}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}^{U}_{Y_{N}},\quad\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}^{U}_{Y^{\varepsilon}_{N}}.

(58)

Then we define

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{N}[U]

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}_{N}[U]+\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}[U]+\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}[U],

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},\varepsilon}_{N}[U]

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{Z}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]+\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U]+\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}^{\varepsilon}_{N}[U],\qquad\varepsilon\in(0,1).

Recalling the notations from Definition
4.8
and
Proposition
4.10
, for
M=M(U,\delta;1)
(see (
55
)) we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}_{W_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{M}[U]},

\displaystyle\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U;W_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{M}[U]),

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{W^{\varepsilon}_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},\varepsilon}_{M}[U]},

\displaystyle\lambda_{k;\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)

\displaystyle\vcentcolon=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U;W^{\varepsilon}_{M},\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},\varepsilon}_{M}[U]).

Theorem 5.8
.

Impose the construction and Neumann assumptions,
3.2

(I)
and
(II)
.
Let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain.
For
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}=-\Delta-\xi_{\varepsilon}+c_{\varepsilon}

(59)

Then, one has (see Definition
1.6
for “
\xrightarrow{\textnormal{NR}}
”)

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\textnormal{NR}}_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}\mbox{ in probability,}

\displaystyle\lambda_{k;\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)\mbox{ in probability,}\qquad k\in\mathbb{N}.

and for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
there exist an eigenfunction
\phi_{k}
of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}
corresponding to
\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)
and eigenfunctions
\phi_{k,m}
of
\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}_{\varepsilon}
corresponding to
\lambda_{k;\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U)
for all
\varepsilon>0
such that

\displaystyle\phi_{k,m}\xrightarrow{m\to\infty}\phi_{k}\quad\mbox{ in }L^{2}(U)\mbox{ in probability}.

Proof.

(
59
) follows by
the construction assumption
3.2

(I)

and Lemma
1.15
(see also the discussion in Section
1.5
and Remark
3.5
).

The convergences follows in a similar as in Theorem
5.3
, that is we again apply Theorem
4.14
and Lemma
4.12
.
The convergence of
W_{n}
and
\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\varepsilon}[U]
follow in the same way as in Theorem
5.3
.
The convergence of
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{N}^{\varepsilon}[U]
is guaranteed by the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
and Theorem
4.3

(b)
and the convergence of

by

4.3

(c)
and

.
∎

Without the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,
we can still construct an artificial Neumann Anderson Hamiltonian,
which will be used in Section

as a technical tool.

Definition 5.9
.

Impose the construction assumption
3.2

(I)

and
let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain and
r\in(1-\delta,1)
.
For
M=M(U,\delta;1)
, we set

\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}\vcentcolon=\mathcal{H}_{W_{M},\mathcal{Z}_{M}[U]}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U},\quad\overline{\lambda}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{k}(U)\vcentcolon=\lambda_{k}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U;W_{M},\mathcal{Z}_{M}[U]).

Remark 5.10
.

Similar to Remark
5.4
, for
\mathfrak{M}
being a random variable with values in
\mathbb{N}_{0}
such that
\mathfrak{M}\geq M(U,\delta,1)
, one has, almost surely,

\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}=\overline{\mathcal{H}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}_{W_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}

and, if one imposes the Neumann condition
3.2

(II)
, then

\mathcal{H}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}={\mathcal{H}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}},U}_{W_{\mathfrak{M}},\mathcal{Z}_{\mathfrak{M}}[U]}
.

5.3
Integrated density of states

The aim of this section is to study the integrated density of states (IDS) associated to
the Anderson Hamiltonian with potential
\xi
. Namely, we prove Theorem
1.10
:

•

Theorem
1.10

(a)
and
(e)
are proven by Theorem
5.26
.

•

Theorem
1.10

(b)
is proven by Theorem
5.29
.

•

Theorem
1.10

(c)
and
(d)
are proven by Theorem
5.30
.

Compared to the previous two sections, this section is rather technical involving quantitative estimates obtained before, especially in Section
4.3
.
Before stating the assumptions for this section, we give some estimates on
M
and the symmetric forms
\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
in the following lemma which will be used later on (they will be reformulated in Remark
5.13
under Assumption
5.12
).

Lemma 5.11
.

Impose the construction assumption
3.2

(I)
.
Let
\delta_{-}\in(0,\delta)
and
\sigma\in(0,\infty)
.
Let
U
be a bounded domain.
We will use the following abbreviation for the
M
defined in (
55
):

M_{L,\delta_{-},\gamma}\vcentcolon=M(U_{L},\delta_{-};\gamma)
.
Recall also the definition
\bm{\mathfrak{a}}
and
\bm{\mathfrak{b}}
from (
16
).

(a)

There exists a
C=C(U,\delta_{-},\sigma)\in(0,\infty)
such that for all
L\geq 1
and
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
one has

M(U_{L},\delta_{-};\gamma)\leq 1+(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\log_{2}\big(C\gamma^{-1}L^{\sigma}\lVert X\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\big).

(60)

(b)

Suppose
U
is a bounded Lipschitz domain.
For every
r\in(1-\delta_{-},1)
,
L\geq 1
and
\gamma\in(0,\infty)
,

\llbracket\mathcal{Z}_{M_{L,\delta_{-},\gamma}}[U_{L}]\rrbracket_{H^{r}(U_{L})}\lesssim_{U,\delta_{-},\delta,\sigma}\gamma^{-(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\bm{\mathfrak{b}}}L^{(2+\bm{\mathfrak{b}}(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1})\sigma}\lVert X\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\bm{\mathfrak{b}}}\bm{\mathfrak{a}}.

(c)

Additionally, impose the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
and that
1/2<\delta_{-}
.
Suppose that
r\in(\frac{3}{2}-\delta_{-},1)
,
\varepsilon\in(0,\delta_{-}-\frac{1}{2})
,
p\in(2,\infty)
and
q\in(1,2)
are such that
\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}=1
and (
41
) holds for
s=r
.
Then, for every
\sigma\in(0,\infty)
,
L\geq 1
and
\gamma\in(0,1]
,

\llbracket\mathcal{Z}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{M_{L,\delta_{-},\gamma}}[U_{L}]\rrbracket_{H^{r}(U_{L})}\\
\lesssim_{U,\delta_{-},\delta,\sigma,\varepsilon}\gamma^{-(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\bm{\mathfrak{b}}}L^{(2+(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\bm{\mathfrak{b}})\sigma}\lVert X\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}^{(\delta-\delta_{-})^{-1}\bm{\mathfrak{b}}}(\bm{\mathfrak{a}}+\lVert\xi\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})})\\
+L^{2\varepsilon}\lVert\tilde{Y}^{U_{L}}\rVert_{B_{p,p}^{-2+\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}+L^{\sigma}\lVert X-\xi\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-2+2\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Proof.

(a)

(
60
) is a direct consequence of Lemma
2.11
.

(b)

follows by
Theorem
4.3

(a)
(see (
39
)) since by definition of
\bm{\mathfrak{b}}
and
\bm{\mathfrak{a}}
(see (
16
)),

\lVert Y_{N}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{-1+\delta_{-},\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\leq 2^{\bm{\mathfrak{b}}N}\bm{\mathfrak{a}},

(61)

and by using (
60
).

For
(c)
we use
(b)
and estimate
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{M_{L,\gamma}}[U_{L}]
and
\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{M_{L,\gamma}}[U_{L}]
. By Theorem
4.3

(b)
and
(c)
we have

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{M_{L,\gamma}}[U_{L}]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta,\varepsilon,p,U}L^{2\varepsilon}\lVert e^{2W_{M_{L,\gamma}}}\rVert_{C^{\delta}(U_{L})}\lVert\tilde{Y}^{U_{L}}\rVert_{B_{p,p}^{-2+\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{Z}}_{M_{L,\gamma}}[U_{L}]

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\delta,\sigma,U}L^{\sigma}\lVert e^{2W_{M_{L,\gamma}}}\rVert_{C^{\delta}(U_{L})}\lVert\widehat{Y}_{M_{L,\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1+{\delta},\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

As for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}
,

\displaystyle|e^{x}-e^{y}|=e^{x}|1-e^{y-x}|\leq Ce^{x}|y-x|\leq Ce^{x\vee y}|y-x|,

by definition of
M_{L,\gamma}
we have

\lVert e^{2W_{M_{L,\gamma}}}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\delta}(U)}\lesssim 2\gamma e^{2\gamma}\leq 2e^{2}
.

Therefore, we obtain the desired inequality by the estimate of
\lVert\widehat{Y}_{M_{L,\gamma}}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{1+\delta,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
from Lemma
5.6
and (
60
).
∎

Lemma 5.16
.

Let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain.
Then, for any
\theta\in(0,1)
, there exist

\lambda_{U,\theta}
,
C_{U,\theta,r}\in(0,\infty)
and an integer
l\in\mathbb{N}
such that for every
\lambda\geq\lambda_{\theta,U}

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)

\displaystyle\geq(1-\theta)\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}\lvert U\rvert\{\lambda+\theta+C_{U,\theta,r}\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{l}\}^{\frac{d}{2}},

(65)

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq(1+\theta)\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}\lvert U\rvert\{\lambda+\theta+C_{U,\theta,r}\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{l}\}^{\frac{d}{2}}.

(66)

In particular,
\mathbb{E}[\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)^{m}]<\infty
for every

and
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
.

If we furthermore impose the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,
then

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)\leq(1+\theta)\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}\lvert U\rvert\{\lambda+\theta+C_{U,\theta,r}(\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert+\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\xi\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert_{\partial U})^{l}\}^{\frac{d}{2}}.

(67)

Proof.

The proof of (
65
) and (
67
) are similar to (
66
), hence we only give the proof of the latter.
Remember that
\bm{N}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
and
\bm{N}_{0}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
are the eigenvalue counting functions of
-\Delta
with Dirichlet and Neumann boundary conditions, respectively.
By Lemma
4.20

(b)
, there exists a
\lambda_{U,\theta}>0
such that
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{U,\theta}
we have

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{0}(U,\lambda)\leq(1+\theta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}\lvert U\rvert\lambda^{\frac{d}{2}}.

Let
\theta^{\prime}\in(0,\infty),\gamma\in(0,1]
.
By Lemma
4.19
, and Remark
5.10
, with
\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}}\vcentcolon=\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}}(W_{M_{\gamma}},\mathcal{Z}_{M_{\gamma}})
where
M_{\gamma}
is the random variable
M(U,\delta;\gamma)
(see (
55
)), one has

\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)\leq(1+\theta)^{\frac{1}{2}}\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}\lvert U\rvert(\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}})^{\frac{d}{2}}.

Recalling the definition of
\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}}
, one observes that there exists a constant
C^{\prime}_{U,\theta^{\prime},r}
such that

\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}}\leq(1+\theta^{\prime})e^{(2+\frac{2r}{1-r})\gamma}(\lambda+\theta^{\prime}+C^{\prime}_{U,\theta^{\prime},r}\llbracket\mathcal{Z}_{M_{\gamma}}\rrbracket_{H^{r}(U)}^{\frac{1}{1-r}}).

Therefore, if
\gamma\vcentcolon=(2+\frac{2r}{1-r})^{-1}\log(1+\theta^{\prime})
and

\theta^{\prime}\vcentcolon=(1+\theta)^{\frac{1}{2d}}-1\in(0,\theta)
, and
C_{U,\theta,r}=C^{\prime}_{U,\theta^{\prime},r}\gamma^{\frac{1-r}{d\sigma}-2}
(see (
62
)), using
(
63
), one has

\displaystyle\Lambda^{+}_{\lambda,\theta^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\theta)^{\frac{1}{d}}(\lambda+\theta+C_{U,\theta,r}\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{l}),

which yields (
66
).
∎

By Lemma
4.19
we derive
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)\leq\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)
and similarly - by using that
\mathcal{Z}_{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}[U]=\mathcal{Z}_{N}[U]
on
H_{0}^{r}
(which follows due to the fact that
\mathcal{T}
equals zero on
H_{0}^{r}(U)
, see Lemma
2.8
) -
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)
.
Therefore, as a direct consequence of Lemma
5.16

we obtain the following asymptotics.
These asymptotics agree with the asymptotics of the eigenvalue counting function for the Laplacian operator, as proven by Weyl (also called Weyl’s law) and later generalised for a class of Schrödinger operators by Kirsch and Martinelli
[
43
, Proposition 2.3]
(observe that our results agree with the work of Mouzard on two dimensional manifolds, see
[
57
]
).

Proposition 5.17
.

Let
U
be a bounded Lipschitz domain, then

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)=\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)=\frac{|B(0,1)|}{(2\pi)^{d}}|U|,

and under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,

\displaystyle\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U,\lambda)=\frac{|B(0,1)|}{(2\pi)^{d}}|U|.

Proposition 5.18
.

There exist (deterministic) functions
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
,
\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
and
\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
, such that for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mathbb{P}
-almost surely and in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
, with
Q=y+[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
,

\displaystyle\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\lim_{L\in\mathbb{Q},L\to\infty}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda),

\displaystyle\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\lim_{L\in\mathbb{Q},L\to\infty}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)

exist. Moreover,

\displaystyle\sup_{L>0}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)]

\displaystyle=\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)=\inf_{L>0}\frac{1}{L^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)].

(68)

Under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,
one may simultaneously replace
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
by
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
and
\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
by
\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
in the above definition and inequality.

Proof.

The existence of the mentioned limits in the
\mathbb{P}
-almost sure sense and the equalities in (
68
) follow by an application of the ergodic theorem by Akcoglu and Krengel
[
1
]
, see also
[
43
, Section 3]
for a similar construction (in case the potential is a function).
The conditions of this ergodic theorem are satisfied on the one hand by the ergodic assumptions
3.2

(III)
and on the other hand by the fact that
Q\mapsto\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q,\lambda)
is superadditive and
Q\mapsto\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q,\lambda)
is subadditive, which follows by Lemma
4.24
and Remark
4.25
.
That the convergences hold also in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
and the inequality in (
68
) holds, follows by Lemma
4.18
as for
Q\subseteq[-1,1]^{d}
, it implies

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q,\lambda)\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([-1,1]^{d},\lambda)\leq\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}([-1,1]^{d},\lambda),

and
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}([-1,1]^{d},\lambda)
is in
L^{1}(\mathbb{P})
by Lemma
5.16
.
∎

Remark 5.19
.

The cube
Q
in Proposition
5.18
does not need to be centered at the origin. This is important in the proof of
Theorem
5.26
.

Definition 5.20
.

We define (deterministic) functions
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}},\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
by

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)\vcentcolon=\inf_{\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda}\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda^{\prime})\quad\mbox{ and }\quad\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)\vcentcolon=\inf_{\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda}\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda^{\prime}).

Note that they are right-continuous functions that satisfy
\lim_{\lambda\rightarrow-\infty}\bm{N}^{\#}(\lambda)=0
for
\#
denoting either

\textstyle\mathrm{D}

or

\textstyle\mathrm{N}

.

Definition 5.21
.

A sequence
(f_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
of increasing functions
\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
, is said to
converge vaguely
to some function
f:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow[0,\infty)
if
f_{n}(\lambda)\rightarrow f(\lambda)
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
that are continuity points of
f
.

Remark 5.22
.

If
\lambda
is a continuity point of
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)
, then

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)=\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)
.

Remark 5.23
.

Observe that by Lemma
4.28

(b)
and (
63
) for
\gamma=1
, for all
\mu>0
and bounded Lipschitz domains
U
, there exists a
C_{U,\mu}>0
such that

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda+\mu)+C_{U,\mu}L^{d-\frac{1}{2}}[1+\max\{\lambda,0\}+\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{\frac{m}{1-r}}]^{\frac{d}{2}},

and under
the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{N}(U_{L},\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda+\mu)+C_{U,\mu}L^{d-\frac{1}{2}}[1+\max\{\lambda,0\}+(\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{m}+\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\xi\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert_{\partial U})^{\frac{1}{1-r}}]^{\frac{d}{2}}.

Proposition 5.24
.

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
and, under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
.

Proof.

Let
\lambda
be a continuity point of both
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
and
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
. By Remark
5.23
applied to
U=[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]^{d}
, by Proposition
5.18
, because
\bm{\mathcal{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)=\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)
and
\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)=\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)
, see Remark
5.22
, we have for all
\mu>0
,

\displaystyle\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)\leq\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda)\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda+\mu),

so that the equality follows as both
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
and
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
are right-continuous.
Under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
,
we can argue similarly with “
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
” instead of “
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
”.
∎

Definition 5.25
.

Thanks to Proposition
5.24
, we may simply write

\bm{N}\vcentcolon=\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(=\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}\quad\mbox{under
the Neumann assumption\penalty\ \ref{assump:all_three_assump}\penalty\ \ref{item:neumann_assump}}).

We call
\bm{N}
the
integrated density of states
(IDS) for the Anderson Hamiltonian with potential
\xi
.

One can see the validity of the above definition from the following two perspectives:
(i)
\bm{N}
is the limit of eigenvalue counting functions on growing domains (Theorem
5.26
) and
(ii)
\bm{N}
is the limit of the IDS associated with mollified noise (Theorem
5.29
).

Theorem 5.26
.

Let
U
be a bounded domain. Then, almost surely,

\lim_{L\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert U_{L}\rvert}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\cdot)=\bm{N}\quad\mbox{vaguely}.

(69)

If
U
is a bounded Lipschitz domain, then

\lim_{L\in\mathbb{N},L\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert U_{L}\rvert}\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(U_{L},\cdot)=\bm{N}\quad\mbox{vaguely}.

(70)

Under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
, one can replace
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
by
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
in (
70
).

Proof.

Firstly, observe that we may assume
U
to be a bounded Lipschitz domain due to the monotonicity of

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)
as a function of
U
.

By Remark
5.23
it suffices to prove (
69
) (also for
\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
be a continuity point of
\bm{N}
.
We set

I_{n}\vcentcolon=\{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\nonscript\>:\nonscript\>\mathopen{}k+[0,1]^{d}\subseteq 2^{n}U\},\quad J_{n}\vcentcolon=\{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\nonscript\>:\nonscript\>\mathopen{}(k+[0,1]^{d})\cap(2^{n}U)\neq\varnothing\}.

By Lemma
4.18
and Lemma
4.24
,

\sum_{k\in I_{n}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(2^{-n}L(k+[0,1]^{d}),\lambda)\leq\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)\leq\sum_{k\in J_{n}}\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(2^{-n}L(k+[0,1]^{d}),\lambda).

Therefore, by Proposition
5.18
and Remark
5.22
,

\frac{\#I_{n}}{2^{dn}\lvert U\rvert}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda)\leq\liminf_{L\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert U_{L}\rvert}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)\leq\limsup_{L\to\infty}\frac{1}{\lvert U_{L}\rvert}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{L},\lambda)\leq\frac{\#J_{n}}{2^{dn}\lvert U\rvert}\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(\lambda).

By Proposition
5.24
, one has
\bm{N}=\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
.
Thus, the proof is complete by letting
n\to\infty
.
∎

Remark 5.27
.

Recall Remark
5.15
. Let
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.
There exist functions
\bm{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}},\overline{\bm{\mathcal{N}}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{\varepsilon}
and
\bm{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
such that analogues statements as in Proposition
5.18
hold.
Then we define
\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda):=\inf_{\lambda^{\prime}>\lambda}\bm{\mathcal{N}}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(\lambda^{\prime})
and similarly
\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{\varepsilon}
and
\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
.
By analogous arguments as in Theorem
5.26
we also have
\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}=\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}_{\varepsilon}
(
=\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}
under the Neumann assumption
3.2

(II)
).
In this case
\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}:=\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
is called the integrated density of states for the Anderson Hamiltonian with potential
\xi_{\varepsilon}-c_{\varepsilon}
.

For the convergence of
\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}
to
\bm{N}
that we show in Theorem
5.29
, we introduce the following auxiliary lemma.

Lemma 5.28
.

Let
\#
denote either

\textstyle\mathrm{D}

or

\textstyle\mathrm{N}

.
For all
L>0
,
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mu>0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda)]\geq\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda-\mu)].

Proof.

First we observe that as
\lambda_{k;\varepsilon}^{\#}(Q_{L})\to\lambda_{k}^{\#}(Q_{L})
in probability for all
k
(by Theorem
5.3
and Theorem
5.8
), the following holds:
For all
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(0,\infty)
with
\varepsilon_{n}\downarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, there exists a subsequence
(\varepsilon_{n_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
and a
\Omega_{1}\subseteq\Omega
of
\mathbb{P}
-probability
1
, such that on
\Omega_{1}
,
\lambda_{k;\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\#}(Q_{L})\to\lambda_{k}^{\#}(Q_{L})
for all
k
, and therefore for all
\mu>0

\displaystyle\liminf_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda)\geq\bm{N}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda-\mu).

Indeed, if
\mu_{1,m}\leq\mu_{2,m}\leq\cdots
and
\mu_{k,m}\rightarrow\mu_{k}
in
\mathbb{R}
as
m\rightarrow\infty
, for all
k\in\mathbb{N}
, then
\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\mu_{k,m}\leq\mu\}}\geq\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\mathbbm{1}_{\{\mu_{k}\leq\mu-\varepsilon\}}
for all
\mu\in\mathbb{R}
and
\varepsilon>0
.

Therefore, for all
(\varepsilon_{n})_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
in
(0,\infty)
with
\varepsilon_{n}\downarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, there exists a subsequence
(\varepsilon_{n_{m}})_{m\in\mathbb{N}}
such that by Fatou’s lemma

\displaystyle\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}_{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda)]\geq\mathbb{E}[\liminf_{m\rightarrow\infty}\bm{N}_{\varepsilon_{n_{m}}}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda)]\geq\mathbb{E}{[\bm{N}^{\#}(Q_{L},\lambda-\mu)]}.

From this the inequality follows.
∎

Theorem 5.29
.

\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}\to\bm{N}
vaguely.

Proof.

Let
Q\vcentcolon=[-1/2,1/2]^{d}
,
L\in\mathbb{N}
and
\mu\in(0,1)
. Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
be a continuity point of

\bm{N}
. By (
68
) (see also Remark
5.22
)

\bm{N}(\lambda)-\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\leq\frac{1}{L^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)-\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)].

By Lemma
5.28
, for all
L\geq 1
and
\mu>0
,

A\vcentcolon=\limsup_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\{\bm{N}(\lambda)-\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\}\leq\frac{1}{L^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda)-\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q_{L},\lambda-\mu)].

Therefore, by (
68
), taking the infimum over
L\geq 1
in the above inequality, we obtain
A\leq\bm{N}(\lambda)-\bm{N}(\lambda-\mu)
for all
\mu>0
. As
\lambda
is a continuity point of
\bm{N}
, it follows that
A\leq 0
.
Similarly,

one can show

\liminf_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\{\bm{N}(\lambda)-\bm{N}_{\varepsilon}(\lambda)\}\geq 0.\qed

Finally, we prove the tail behavior of the IDS.

Theorem 5.30
.

One has the following tail estimates of the IDS.

(a)

One has
\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}(\lambda)=\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}
.

(b)

For every bounded domain
U
and every
\alpha\in(0,\infty)
, one has

\displaystyle\limsup_{\lambda\to-\infty}(-\lambda)^{-\alpha}\log\bm{N}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\limsup_{\lambda\to-\infty}(-\lambda)^{-\alpha}\log\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)\leq\lambda),

(71)

\displaystyle\liminf_{\lambda\to-\infty}(-\lambda)^{-\alpha}\log\bm{N}(\lambda)

\displaystyle=\liminf_{\lambda\to-\infty}(-\lambda)^{-\alpha}\log\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)\leq\lambda).

(72)

Proof.

(a) Let
Q\vcentcolon=[0,1]^{d}
.
By applying Fatou’s lemma
and the first inequality of (
68
), we obtain

\mathbb{E}[\liminf_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q,\lambda)]\leq\liminf_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}(\lambda)\leq\limsup_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}(\lambda).

Since
\lim_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(Q,\lambda)=\frac{\lvert B(0,1)\rvert}{(2\pi)^{d}}

by Proposition
5.17
, the lower bound is obtained. To obtain the upper bound,
by the last inequality of (
68
) and
the estimate (
66
) from Lemma
5.16
, for any
\theta\in(0,1)
we have

\displaystyle\limsup_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\bm{N}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}\mathbb{E}[\overline{\bm{N}}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{N}$}}}(Q,\lambda)]

\displaystyle\leq\limsup_{\lambda\to\infty}\lambda^{-\frac{d}{2}}(1+\theta)\frac{|B(0,1)|}{(2\pi)^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\{\lambda+\theta+C_{Q,\theta,r}\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\kern-1.07639pt\lvert\bm{\xi}\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert\kern-1.07639pt\rvert^{l}\}^{\frac{d}{2}}]

\displaystyle\leq(1+\theta)\frac{|B(0,1)|}{(2\pi)^{d}}.

Now the identity in (a) follows.

(b) Let
\lambda<0
.
Thanks to the monotonicity of
\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}
(see Proposition
4.10
), we may and do assume

U=[0,L]^{d}
for some
L\in(0,\infty)
.
By (
68
) (see also Remark
5.22
),

\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U)\leq\lambda)\leq\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U,\lambda)]\leq L^{d}\bm{N}(\lambda).

Therefore, we establish that the left-hand sides are greater or equal to the right-hand sides of (
71
) and (
72
).
By Lemma
4.28

(a)
, for
l\in(0,L/2)
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
, one has

\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{n},\lambda)\leq\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}^{d}\cap[-1,n+1]^{d}}\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(k+[-l,L+l]^{d},\lambda+Kl^{-2})

and hence

\frac{1}{n^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}(U_{n},\lambda)]\leq\frac{(n+2)^{d}}{n^{d}}\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d},\lambda+Kl^{-2})].

Letting
n\to\infty
, for
p,q\in(1,\infty)
with
p^{-1}+q^{-1}=1
, one obtains

\displaystyle\bm{N}(\lambda)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d},\lambda+Kl^{-2})]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d},\lambda+Kl^{-2})\mathbbm{1}_{\{\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d})\leq\lambda+Kl^{-2}\}}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d},Kl^{-2})^{q}]^{\frac{1}{q}}\mathbb{P}(\lambda_{1}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d})\leq\lambda+Kl^{-2})^{\frac{1}{p}}.

where we applied Hölder’s inequality in the second inequality.
Note that

\mathbb{E}[\bm{N}^{\mathchoice{\scalebox{0.8}{$\displaystyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\textstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}{\scalebox{0.8}{$\scriptscriptstyle\mathrm{D}$}}}([0,L+2l]^{d},Kl^{-2})^{q}]<\infty

by Lemma
4.19

and Lemma
4.20

(b)
.
Therefore, for
U=[0,L+2l]^{d}
, the left-hand sides are less or equal to the right-hand sides of (
71
) and (
72
).
As
L
and
l\in(0,L/2)
can be chosen arbitrarily, the equalities follow.
∎

Appendix A
Estimates related to function spaces

A.1
Estimates in Besov spaces

This subsection gives estimates in weighted Besov spaces (see Definition
2.2
).

Lemma A.1
.

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
,
r\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\in[0,\infty)
. Then

\displaystyle\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r,\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,r,\sigma_{1}}\lVert f\rVert_{B_{p,\infty}^{r+\frac{d}{p},\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},

(73)

\displaystyle\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r,\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{p,q,r,\kappa,\sigma_{1}}\lVert f\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r+\frac{d}{p}+\kappa,\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad\kappa>0.

(74)

If
p\sigma_{2}>d
, then

\lVert f\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,r,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r,\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

(75)

Proof.

By
[
71
, Theorem 6.5]
, one has
\lVert f\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\sim_{p,q,r,\sigma}\lVert w_{\sigma}f\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
.
Therefore (
73
) and (
74
) follow by the (unweighted) Besov embedding, see
[
70
, Section 2.7.1]
.
For (
75
), the product estimate in the Besov space (see
[
51
, Corollary 2.1.35]
, which follows also from
[
59
, Lemma 2.1]
)
yields

\lVert f\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma_{1}+\sigma_{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,r,\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}}\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{B^{\lvert r\rvert+\frac{1}{2}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert f\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r,\sigma_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Now
\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{B^{\lvert r\rvert+\frac{1}{2}}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{B^{\lvert r\rvert+\frac{3}{4}}_{p,p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{W^{\lvert r\rvert+\frac{3}{4}}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{W^{\lvert r\rvert+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}
(by Lemma
2.7
).
Since
\lvert\partial^{m}w_{\sigma_{2}}\rvert\lesssim_{m,\sigma_{2}}w_{\sigma_{2}-\lvert m\rvert}
,
we have
\lVert w_{\sigma_{2}}\rVert_{W^{\lvert r\rvert+1}_{p}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}<\infty
if
p\sigma_{2}>d
.
∎

Theorem A.2

(Weighted Young’s inequality)
.

Let
p,q,r\in[1,\infty]
,
\frac{1}{r}+1=\frac{1}{p}+\frac{1}{q}
and
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
. Then

\displaystyle\|w_{\sigma}(f*g)\|_{L^{r}}\lesssim_{\sigma}\|w_{-\sigma}f\|_{L^{p}}\|w_{\sigma}g\|_{L^{q}}.

Proof.

Using that
w_{\sigma}(x)\lesssim_{\sigma}w_{\sigma}(x-y)w_{-\sigma}(y)
, one can estimate
w_{\sigma}(x)|f*g|(x)\leq[(|f|w_{-\sigma})*(|g|w_{\sigma})](x)
, see also
[
56
, Theorem 2.4]
. The rest follows by Young’s inequality,
[
4
, Theorem 1.4]
.
∎

Theorem A.3
.

Let
r\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma\in[0,\infty]
.
Let
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\int\varphi=1
and
\varphi_{\varepsilon}(x)=\varepsilon^{-d}\varphi(\varepsilon^{-1}x)
.
Then, for all
\eta\in\mathcal{C}^{r,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\eta-\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r-\delta,\sigma+\delta}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}0.

Proof.

In this proof we refrain from writing “
(\mathbb{R}^{d})
”.
Let
\delta>0
and
p\in(\frac{d}{\delta},\infty)
.
By Lemma
A.1
, (
75
),
\eta
is an element of
B_{p,1}^{r,\sigma+\delta}
.
As by Lemma
A.1
, (
73
),

\displaystyle\lVert\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\eta-\eta\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{r-\delta,\sigma+\delta}}\lesssim\lVert\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\eta-\eta\rVert_{B_{p,1}^{r,\sigma+\delta}}=\sum_{j=-1}^{\infty}2^{rj}\|w_{\sigma+\delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\Delta_{j}\eta-\Delta_{j}\eta)\|_{L^{p}}.

It suffices to show for all
j
that
\|w_{\sigma+\delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\Delta_{j}\eta-\Delta_{j}\eta)\|_{L^{p}}\xrightarrow{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}0
and

\displaystyle\|w_{\sigma+\delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\Delta_{j}\eta-\Delta_{j}\eta)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim\|w_{\sigma+\delta}\Delta_{j}\eta\|_{L^{p}}

(76)

As
\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\Delta_{j}\eta
converges to
\Delta_{j}\eta
almost everywhere (as it does at every Lebesgue point, see
[
40
, Proposition 2.3.8]
),
the converges follows from (
76
) by Lebesgue’s dominated convergence theorem.
By the weighted Young inequality, we have
\|w_{\sigma+\delta}(\varphi_{\varepsilon}*\Delta_{j}\eta)\|_{L^{p}}\lesssim_{\sigma}\|w_{-(\sigma+\delta)}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\|w_{\sigma+\delta}\Delta_{j}\eta\|_{L^{p}}
. As
w_{-(\sigma+\delta)}(\varepsilon x)\leq w_{-(\sigma+\delta)}(x)
for
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
, we have
\|w_{-(\sigma+\delta)}\varphi_{\varepsilon}\|_{L^{1}}\leq\|w_{-(\sigma+\delta)}\varphi\|_{L^{1}}
which is finite because
\varphi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. This proves (
76
).
∎

Lemma A.4
.

Let
p,q\in[1,\infty]
,

r\in\mathbb{R}
and
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
. Let
Z\in B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and

\phi\in\mathcal{S}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then, one has

\lVert\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)Z\rVert_{B^{r}_{p,q}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,r,\sigma,\phi}L^{\sigma}\lVert Z\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})},\qquad L\geq 1.

Proof.

By the product estimate for Besov spaces
[
63
, Theorem 4.37]
, we have

\lVert\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)Z\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,r}\lVert\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)w_{-\sigma}\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{\lvert r\rvert+\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lVert w_{\sigma}Z\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}.

Since
\lVert w_{\sigma}Z\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{p,q,r,\sigma}\lVert Z\rVert_{B_{p,q}^{r,\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}

by
[
71
, Theorem 6.5]
and
\lVert\cdot\rVert_{\mathcal{C}^{|r|+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\lVert\cdot\rVert_{W_{\infty}^{|r|+\frac{1}{2}}}\lesssim\lVert\cdot\rVert_{W_{\infty}^{|r|+1}}
by Lemma
2.7
, it suffices to show

\lVert\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)w_{-\sigma}\rVert_{W_{\infty}^{\lvert r\rvert+1}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{r,\sigma,\phi}L^{\sigma}.

For this, it suffices to show

\lVert\partial^{m}[w_{-\sigma}\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)]\rVert_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}\lesssim_{\sigma,m,\phi}L^{\sigma}

for every
m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}^{d}
.
By the Leibniz rule,

\partial^{m}[w_{-\sigma}\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)]=\sum_{l=0}^{m}\binom{m}{l}L^{-\lvert m-l\rvert}\partial^{l}w_{-\sigma}\partial^{m-l}\phi(L^{-1}\cdot).

Since
\lvert\partial^{l}w_{-\sigma}(x)\rvert\lesssim_{\sigma,l}(1+\lvert x\rvert^{2})^{\frac{\sigma-\lvert l\rvert}{2}}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\lvert\partial^{m}[w_{-\sigma}\phi(L^{-1}\cdot)](x)\rvert
